### `T5-01` - The TLS fingerprint: JA3/JA4 and the ClientHello features

- **Status:** done — **Claimed by:** claude-opus-5[1m] — **Session:** ses_t5_01 — **Blocked by:** —
- **Files:** `src/dpi_engine/tls_fingerprint.py` (new, 281 statements), `tests/test_tls_fingerprint.py` (new, 65 tests).
- **Test added:** 65 tests, built from **independent bytes** rather than from the implementation: the two worked JA3 examples in the `salesforce/ja3` README, FoxIO's JA4 example (its raw sorted lists asserted separately, so a failure names the part rather than the hash), and a ServerHello whose extension pair `0x002b,0x0033` hashes to `a56c5b993250` — the value in FoxIO's published Sliver/SoftEther JA4S — so `t130200_1301_a56c5b993250` matches end to end. All eight of JA4.md's ALPN examples are pinned. **100% statement and 100% branch coverage** of the new module; 25/25 mutants caught.
- **How it was tackled:** One parse, five readings. The parser walks the record, the handshake and the extension block once, bounds-checking every length prefix, and keeps the lists GREASE-free: JA3 is the MD5 of `version,ciphers,extensions,curves,point-formats`; JA4 is the readable `a` part plus two hashes over the **sorted** cipher list and the **sorted** extension list (SNI and ALPN excluded, signature algorithms appended in arrival order); JA4S is the same shape over the server's **arrival-ordered** extension list, because a server's ordering is the signal. GREASE is removed from all four lists — including the values *inside* `supported_groups` and `supported_versions`, where the largest value would otherwise decide the JA4 version — and the removed values are returned, so "no GREASE" and "GREASE present and ignored" are different facts. The features are typed fields because a signature needs the ordering, not only the hash. **The contract is stricter than `extract_sni` on purpose:** a hello that does not parse completely returns `None`, and a hello split across two records is refused rather than reassembled, because a hash over half a cipher list is a wrong fact that looks exactly like a real one and `T5-04` will treat an exact match as evidence. Two of my own expectations were wrong and the vectors caught them: `0x30`/`0x31` **are** alphanumeric, so JA4.md's `01` was never a contradiction, and an empty signature-algorithm extension hashes `000d_` (`176fa2dad4eb`), not `_`.
- **Deviations:** five, all in §7 — the plan's "(S/M/I)" names no published variant set (JA3/JA3S and JA4/JA4S implemented, JA4H/JA4X left to `T5-02`); the tracker's one-line JA3 description counted four lists where the published method hashes five; strict refusal replaces partial parsing; the ALPN field follows JA4.md's documented hex rule, which differs from FoxIO's Python reference for non-ASCII endpoint bytes; and **JA4S is patent-pending under the FoxIO License 1.1**, which permits internal use but needs an OEM licence for monetisation, where JA3 and JA4 are BSD-3-Clause.
- **Verification:** `uv run ruff check .` clean — `uv run mypy` clean on **49 source files** — `uv run pytest -q` → **1446 passed, 1 skipped** — total coverage **92.64%**, `dpi_engine.tls_fingerprint` at **100% statements / 100% branches** — mutation harness **25/25 caught**, 0 survived, 0 stale, source restored byte-for-byte.
- **Closed findings:** none.
- **Follow-ups:** `T5-03` is the first consumer — nothing calls this module yet, so it is an engine capability with no pipeline wiring, as the decomposition intends. JA4H/JA4X are `T5-02`'s. The QUIC (`q`) and DTLS (`d`) prefixes are in the version table but unreachable, because only TLS over TCP is parsed. A ClientHello split across records yields no fingerprint until reassembly exists. The ALPN divergence from tshark applies only to non-alphanumeric ALPN values, which no IANA-registered protocol uses.
- **Notes:** Corrected: JA3 hashes **five** lists (version, ciphers, extensions, elliptic curves, EC point formats) and JA3S three; JA4 does not hash "the same lists in the same order" — it hashes sorted lists, excludes SNI and ALPN from the extension material, and appends the signature algorithms. The features are the lists themselves, because a signature needs the ordering and not only the hash; GREASE is removed before every hash and returned separately.

### `T5-02` - The certificate, SNI-absence and HTTP/2 features

- **Status:** done — **Claimed by:** claude-opus-5[1m] — **Session:** ses_t5_02 — **Blocked by:** —
- **Files:** `src/dpi_engine/tls_certificate.py` (new, 221 statements), `src/dpi_engine/http2.py` (new, 38 statements), `src/dpi_engine/tls_fingerprint.py` (`sni_present`), `tests/test_tls_certificate.py` (new, 78 tests), `tests/test_http2.py` (new, 20 tests), `tests/test_tls_fingerprint.py` (+2).
- **Test added:** **100** — 78 for the certificate and 20 for HTTP/2, built against bytes generated **outside** the parser: three real X.509 certificates (a self-signed RSA CA, an RSA leaf with a six-RDN subject, and an ECDSA leaf whose UTF-8 name carries a comma, a semicolon and a multi-byte `emailAddress` OID, with a UTCTime `notBefore` and a GeneralizedTime `notAfter`) produced once with `cryptography`/OpenSSL and embedded as base64 — the expected names and dates are what **OpenSSL reported for those exact bytes**, not what this parser produced. The published HTTP/2 string is pinned as Chrome's `1:65536;2:0;3:1000;4:6291456;6:262144`. Then the shapes a real CA cannot issue: 30 hand-built DER variants (one per refusal), every prefix of the stream and of the certificate, every fragment boundary of a message spanning two records, a four-byte length DER forbids, an element that overruns its parent **while the bytes exist later in the buffer**, and RFC 4514 escaping and `#` hex-form rendering. Two `sni_present` cases in `test_tls_fingerprint.py`, plus the property test's new invariant `sni_present == (SNI in extensions)`. **100% statement and 100% branch coverage** of both new modules; **40/40 mutants caught**.
- **How it was tackled:** Three contracts, each strict in the same way. **The certificate** is read from the server's plaintext handshake: the record walker accumulates handshake messages across record boundaries (a Certificate message may be fragmented) and stops at the first record that is not a handshake record, because from there the stream is a change of cipher state or ciphertext — and a Certificate-shaped payload inside an *encrypted* record is refused by content type, not believed because it parses. Only the leaf is interpreted; the DER reader is hand-rolled (no new runtime dependency) and bounded: definite lengths only, at most three length bytes, no high-tag-number form, and every child checked against its **enclosing** bound rather than the buffer's — the two differ, and a test builds a certificate where they do. Names are rendered in DER order, RFC 4514-escaped, with multi-valued RDNs joined by `+` and a documented short-name table; an unmapped OID stays dotted and a non-string value uses `#` hex, whose own `#` is syntax and is therefore not escaped. Times are aware UTC datetimes read from either encoding, with RFC 5280's two-digit window — **and the window test found a real bug**: `1950 + yy` instead of `1900 + yy`, which dated 1950 as 2000. **HTTP/2** checks the 24-byte preface and the first frame: SETTINGS, stream 0, no ACK, a whole number of six-byte entries, all inside the buffer — then renders the entries in arrival order, because two clients that send the same settings in a different order are different clients. **SNI presence** is a new `sni_present` field on `ClientHelloFeatures`, set from the parser's own `has_sni`, so "sent the extension with an empty name list" and "sent nothing" are different facts even though `extract_sni` answers `None` for both. Writing the test also exposed a defect in `T5-01`'s fixture: `_sni_body` carried a spurious per-entry length that RFC 6066 does not define, so it was corrected against `extract_sni`; no published vector moved, because no hash covers the SNI body.
- **Deviations:** three, all in §7 — TLS 1.3's Certificate message is ciphertext, so `None` means "not visible" and not "absent"; only the SETTINGS half of the published Akamai HTTP/2 string is produced; and the SNI-absence fact lives on `ClientHelloFeatures`, with the flow row's wiring left to `T5-03`.
- **Verification:** `uv run ruff check .` clean — `uv run mypy` clean on **51 source files** — `uv run pytest -q` → **1522 passed, 25 skipped** — total coverage **93.07%**, `dpi_engine.tls_certificate` at **100% statements / 100% branches** and `dpi_engine.http2` at **100% / 100%** — mutation harness **40/40 caught**, 0 survived, 0 stale, all three sources restored byte-for-byte. The 24 extra skips are the C++ differential suite (`C++ reference toolchain unavailable`), which ran and passed in `ses_phase4`; the 25th is the opt-in quality run. Passed count moved 1446 → 1522: **+100 tests, −24 that now skip**.
- **Closed findings:** none.
- **Follow-ups:** `T5-03` is the first consumer — nothing calls either module yet, so the engine gained capability without wiring, as the decomposition intends. A TLS 1.3 certificate needs keys and is out of reach of a passive capture; a decrypted stream is where that path would land. The HTTP/2 fingerprint stops at SETTINGS; WINDOW_UPDATE and PRIORITY belong to whoever needs the full Akamai string. The certificate fields are facts, not verdicts: no chain validation, no signature check and no expiry judgement — `T5-04` is where a date becomes evidence.
- **Notes:** The rest of the plan's feature list. SNI-absence is a feature and not a gap: a client that sends no SNI is telling you something, and a classifier that cannot see it will call every such flow unknown. The leaf's validity is reported **as encoded**, so an inverted window is a returned fact rather than a refusal — a certificate that expired, or that never became valid, is exactly what a classifier wants to see.

### `T5-03` - The per-flow feature vector

- **Status:** done — **Claimed by:** claude-opus-5[1m] — **Session:** ses_t5_03 — **Blocked by:** —
- **Files:** `src/dpi_engine/flow_features.py` (new, 142 statements), `src/dpi_engine/types.py` (`Connection.features`, `FlowRecord.features`), `src/dpi_engine/worker.py` (the per-packet observation), `src/dpi_engine/connection_tracker.py` (`to_flow_records`), `src/dpi_engine/run_store.py` (`SCHEMA` v2 and the row codec), `src/dpi_engine/tls_fingerprint.py` (the ServerHello `supported_versions` fix, below), `tests/test_flow_features.py` (new, 65 tests), `tests/test_run_store.py` (+4), `tests/test_tls_fingerprint.py` (+1 and three server-side vectors corrected), `tests/test_tools_run_store.py` (the pinned row key set), `tests/test_flow_table.py`, `tests/test_agent_verdicts.py`.
- **Test added:** **70** — 65 in `tests/test_flow_features.py`, 4 for the store's v2 schema, 1 for the ServerHello regression. The new file is three layers thick on purpose: **the observation** (one payload in, a vector out — with the certificate's names and dates taken from **OpenSSL's** report on those DER bytes, imported rather than copied so the two cannot drift, and Chrome's published `1:65536;2:0;3:1000;4:6291456;6:262144`), **the row** (`Connection` -> `to_flow_records` -> `FlowRecord` -> `flow_to_json`/`flow_from_json`, including a refused row that has no `features` key), and **the run** (four synthetic pcaps through `run_pipeline`, which is the only layer that can catch a hot path that never calls the observer). The builders write the TLS framing here, with an extension order that is deliberately not ascending so a sorting bug cannot pass, and the client half is also driven by FoxIO's published JA4 example so one end-to-end assertion is an external value. Refusals are one test each: a certificate record with no ServerHello in the buffer, a hello split across records, a truncated flight, an impossible record version, `bool` as an integer, a naive timestamp, a three-element version pair, a wrong-shaped certificate. A property test feeds 200 arbitrary byte strings and asserts the observer never raises, never invents a hash of the wrong length, and always round-trips. **100% statement and 100% branch coverage** of `flow_features.py`; **34/34 mutants caught**.
- **How it was tackled:** The vector is a **carrier**, and the carrier is the whole task: `T5-01` and `T5-02` had no consumer, so a flow's row is what turns them from contracts into facts a signature can match after the run. `FlowFeatures` is frozen and holds the ten ClientHello fields, the five ServerHello fields, the certificate and `http2_settings` — and deliberately **not** a cipher count or an extension count, because those are `len()` of lists that are already there and JA4's `a` part already encodes them; two homes for one number is how a signature starts matching a number that disagrees with its evidence. ALPN tokens are decoded **latin-1**, the one encoding that maps every byte to a code point and back, so no token becomes `?` and a JSON round trip loses nothing. **Direction is the hard part**, because a payload does not say which side sent it: a certificate is attributed only when the **same buffer** also carries the ServerHello, so a client's `Certificate` message in mutual TLS can never be read as the server's — a wrong fact that looks exactly like a right one. Merging is **first observation wins per half**, not per field: a retransmitted hello is identical and a renegotiation must not rewrite how the connection opened, while mixing a cipher list from one hello with an ALPN offer from another would describe a client that never existed. `ja3` and `ja3s` being non-empty are the halves' presence flags. The store moved to **`dpi-run/v2`** and `flow_from_json` is **strict**: a v1 record is refused by version, and a row that claims v2 but lacks the key is refused by the decoder, so nothing can be read as "observed nothing". **Wiring the server half found a real defect in `T5-01`:** `_scan_extensions` read the ServerHello's `supported_versions` with the ClientHello's 1-byte-length-prefixed vector form, but RFC 8446 section 4.2.1 gives the server the selected version as a bare uint16 — so **every conformant TLS 1.3 ServerHello returned `None`**, which is most traffic, while the TLS 1.2 case (no such extension) parsed fine. Fixed with a server-mode parse that requires exactly two bytes; the published Sliver JA4S still matches, and it now matches on RFC-correct bytes rather than on a fixture built to the bug.
- **Deviations:** two, both reachability facts in §7 — the certificate needs the ServerHello **and** the Certificate in the same packet, and the HTTP/2 SETTINGS string is populated for cleartext h2c only. The `sni_present` fact that `T5-02` left unwired is on the row now, which closes that deviation. The `dpi-run/v1` -> `v2` move is the task's stated gate rather than a deviation.
- **Verification:** `uv run ruff check .` clean — `uv run mypy` clean on **52 source files** — `uv run pytest -q` → **1592 passed, 25 skipped** — total coverage **93.21%**, `dpi_engine.flow_features` at **100% statements / 100% branches**, `run_store`, `tls_certificate` and `tls_fingerprint` still at **100%** — mutation harness **34/34 caught**, 0 survived, 0 stale, all five sources restored byte-for-byte. Passed count moved 1522 -> 1592: **+70, none removed**. The 24 differential skips are the C++ reference toolchain; the 25th is the opt-in quality run.
- **Closed findings:** none.
- **Follow-ups:** **TCP reassembly is now the binding constraint**, and the two §7 rows are its symptoms: a ClientHello split across segments, and a server flight whose Certificate spans packets, both yield "not observed". That is one task, not two, and it would make the certificate reachable for real chains. Over TLS the HTTP/2 SETTINGS frame stays ciphertext until there is a decrypted stream. `T5-04` is the first consumer: it matches on this vector, and `get_fingerprints` — the Layer-2 tool the architecture names — can now be answered from the row instead of from the packets.
- **Notes:** `http2_settings` is `str | None` and not `str` on purpose: `None` is "no SETTINGS frame was observed" and `""` is the fingerprint of a frame with no entries, and collapsing them would let a flow read as fingerprinted when it was not — the same trap `sni_present` exists to avoid. The codec lives with the vector rather than in the store because the shape is the vector's, and both directions are strict. The certificate is a fact, not a verdict: there is still no chain validation, no signature check and no expiry judgement, and `T5-04`/`T5-05` are where a date becomes evidence.

### `T5-04` - The signature table: a format, a matcher, and the corpus's known apps

- **Status:** done — **Claimed by:** claude-opus-5[1m] — **Session:** ses_t5_04 — **Blocked by:** —
- **Files:** `src/dpi_engine/signatures.py` (new, 143 statements), `tests/test_signatures.py` (new, 96 tests). No existing module changed: the table is a pure matcher over `T5-03`'s vector, and nothing calls it yet.
- **Test added:** **96** — in `tests/test_signatures.py`, across four layers. **The format:** a signature round-trips through its JSON codec, the shipped table survives its own codec, and every `AppType` name is a legal app. **The matcher:** scalar equality, list membership, the version projection, an unobserved certificate, SNI-`False` versus no-hello, table order, repeatability, two signatures agreeing on one app, and the conflict that must answer unknown. **The refusals:** every missing key, every unknown key at all three levels, a non-list `all_of`/`signatures`, non-object entries, a blank id/app, an empty conjunction, a duplicate predicate, a duplicate id, identical predicate sets under any app, a version that is not an integer (including `True` and `1.0`), and a value of the wrong type for every scalar and list field. **The shipped table:** the published Chrome JA4 and FoxIO's published Sliver JA4S identify, while a flow that showed nothing and **all 59 corpus flows across 33 cases** abstain. Fixtures are imported from `test_tls_fingerprint.py`, `test_flow_features.py` and `test_tls_certificate.py` rather than restated, so the table is checked against bytes this file did not build. **100% statement and 100% branch coverage** of `signatures.py`; **52/52 mutants caught**, one proven equivalent.
- **How it was tackled:** A signature is a **conjunction over one `FlowFeatures`**, and the conjunction is the task: a JA4 hash answers "the same client as that flow" and cannot answer "why", which is the wrong shape for `T5-05` to propose and `T5-06` to falsify. Every matchable field is one field of the vector or one of the two certificate names, so there is no second home for a fact; the six list fields match by **membership**, and `tls_versions` is projected to the versions offered because the counts are a client-specific tally that JA3/JA4 already encode. Three absences are deliberate: **no negation**, because a "matches everything except X" signature is a category and would match a flow that showed nothing; **no empty value**, because `""`/`null` are what an unobserved field reads as, which is also why `http2_settings: ""` is not matchable; and **no unobserved match**, so `certificate.issuer` cannot match a TLS 1.3 flow whose certificate is ciphertext, while `sni_present: false` stays a positive fact and `None` (no ClientHello) equals neither boolean. The matcher has **no score, no ranking and no fallback**: if every matching signature agrees, that app is the answer; if they disagree, the answer is unknown with the ids in `matched`, because choosing by table order or specificity would be a coin flip dressed as evidence — and the gate this task implements is exactly "unknown rather than nearest". The loader is strict in the same spirit: unknown keys, duplicate predicates, duplicate ids and **identical predicate sets under any app** are refused, since that table can never mean what it says. **The invariants live in `Signature`/`SignatureTable.__post_init__`, not only in the loader**, because `T5-05` builds signatures in memory and a table that is honest only through one door is not honest; the mutation harness is what put them there, and it also caught the one survivor: an `all_of` **dict** was refused only because its keys were not objects, so the test now pins "must be a list" rather than merely "raises". `DEFAULT_TABLE` ships **two signatures this repository has published evidence for** — JA4.md's worked Chrome example and FoxIO's published Sliver JA4S — each a single predicate because a JA4 *is* a conjunction of version, cipher and extension facts; a SETTINGS-only signature is deliberately absent, because Chrome's SETTINGS string is real but shared, which makes it supporting evidence rather than an identification.
- **Deviations:** two, both in §7 — the shipped table derives **nothing** from the corpus (every corpus flow is one synthetic hello, so a corpus-derived signature would identify the generator), and `app` is an **open string** rather than an `AppType`, because the apps `T5-05` will name are the ones the engine does not know yet.
- **Verification:** `uv run ruff check .` clean — `uv run mypy` clean on **53 source files** — `uv run pytest -q` → **1688 passed, 25 skipped** — total coverage **93.43%**, `dpi_engine.signatures` at **100% statements / 100% branches** — mutation harness **52/52 caught**, 0 survived, 0 stale, the file restored byte-for-byte, and one mutant proven equivalent (the `bool()` around a scalar comparison, which already returns a `bool` for every value the vocabulary admits). Passed count moved 1592 -> 1688: **+96, none removed**.
- **Closed findings:** none.
- **Follow-ups:** `T5-05` is the first consumer — it proposes signatures and can already test a candidate with `Signature.matches` against a single flow, which is the shape `T5-06`'s Critic needs to falsify one. Nothing in the pipeline calls this module yet and the table is not persisted anywhere, so the JSON codec is the contract for whatever stores proposals. A signature should describe **one side** of a connection: a client and a server fingerprint in one conjunction claim that both ends belong to the same app, which is usually false, and that warning is in the module docstring where the next author will read it. The `app` namespace is open: every `AppType` name is legal and test-pinned, but nothing yet maps a proposed label onto a `FlowRecord.app`.
- **Notes:** `DEFAULT_TABLE` is evidence, not coverage: two signatures is a table that abstains on almost everything, and that is the honest state the next task starts from. The certificate fields are strings from the DER, so a match on an issuer is a match on the rendering `T5-02` defined, not on the encoding. `http2_settings: ""` (a SETTINGS frame with no entries) is refused as a predicate value for the same reason `T5-03` kept it distinct from `None`.

### `T5-05` - The `ClassifierGap` agent: an unknown app, correlated and proposed

- **Status:** done — **Claimed by:** claude-opus-5[1m] — **Session:** ses_t5_05 — **Blocked by:** —
- **Files:** `src/dpi_engine/proposals.py` (new: `common_predicates`); `src/dpi_engine/signatures.py`
  (the vocabulary and the predicate codec become public: `SCALAR_FIELDS`, `LIST_FIELDS`,
  `observed_fields`, `predicate_from_json`, `predicate_to_json`); `src/dpi_agents/schemas.py`
  (`NewAppCandidate`, `parse_new_app_candidate`); `src/dpi_agents/nodes/classifier_gap.py` (new);
  `tests/test_classifier_gap.py` (new); `tests/test_signatures.py` (follows the rename).
- **Test added:** **66** tests in `tests/test_classifier_gap.py`, across the task's three layers:
  the engine's conjunction (agreement, list intersection, canonical order, absence, the two
  refusals), the claim (`NewAppCandidate`'s own invariants and the parser's evidence check), and
  the node against a **real MCP server** (tool list, prompt vocabulary, and the integration loop
  over a corpus-built capture). Every new path is at 100% statement and branch coverage.
- **How it was tackled:** **The gap has a deterministic half and a judgement half, and they are
  kept apart.** `common_predicates` computes what a set of unknown flows actually shares: a scalar
  only when **every** flow agrees on an **observed** value, a list element only when every flow
  lists it, with scalars in vocabulary order and list values sorted so two runs over the same flows
  produce the same tuple. `None` and `""` are absences and propose nothing; `sni_present: false` is
  observed, because sending no SNI is something the client did. An empty set and any already-classified
  flow are **refused**, not filtered: the empty conjunction matches every flow, and a known flow is
  not part of the gap. The model's half is the name and the selection, and `parse_new_app_candidate`
  recomputes `common_predicates` over the **cited** flows and refuses any pattern not in it — so a
  plausible fingerprint the bytes do not show cannot be proposed, however confidently it is phrased.
  The invariants live in `NewAppCandidate.__post_init__` as well as in the parser, for `T5-04`'s
  reason: `T5-06` builds candidates in memory, and an invariant that holds through only one door is
  not an invariant. The node is read-only (`query_flows` only), and its prompt states the tools it
  does **not** have rather than leaving their silence to read as a clean result; `PREDICATE_HELP` is
  asserted at import time to explain exactly the matchable fields.
- **Deviations:** two, both recorded in §7: the design's web-search, ASN and `get_fingerprints` tools
  do not exist in this build, so the node reads the features off the row and says so; and the "any
  unknown app" input is narrower than the plan implies, because a non-empty unrecognised SNI is
  classified `HTTPS` by design — a genuine gap is a no-SNI client on a port nothing guesses.
- **Verification:** `ruff check .` clean · `mypy --strict` clean on **55** source files ·
  `pytest -q` **1754 passed, 25 skipped** · total coverage **93.63%** · `proposals.py`,
  `classifier_gap.py` and the new schema paths at **100%** statement and branch coverage ·
  mutation harness `C:\Users\atuls\AppData\Local\Temp\opencode\mutate_t505.py`:
  **42/42 killed, 0 survivors, 0 stale**, all three targets restored byte-for-byte, with four
  equivalences documented (the NaN guard is redundant with the range check; the parser's name-type
  and evidence-emptiness checks duplicate `__post_init__`; the import-time vocabulary assert
  duplicates the test). Two survivors forced real test strengthenings: `to_signature`'s pattern
  list was only matched for prefix behaviour, and `proposed_patterns: {}` was accepted as "not a
  list" for the wrong reason.
- **Closed findings:** none.
- **Follow-ups:** `T5-06` consumes these candidates — nothing yet **persists** a proposal, writes a
  table entry, or maps a proposed label onto `FlowRecord.app`. The Critic, and therefore the
  falsification half of the loop, is `T5-06`'s. JA4H/JA4X remain out of scope.
- **Notes:** The plan's design says the agent correlates the features with the ASN, the SNI and the
  certificate. This build correlates the features **on the row** and refuses everything else: there
  is no ASN source and no fingerprint tool, so the honest version of that sentence is the prompt's
  "say what the bytes show". The proposal carries citations because a signature nobody can check is
  a rule nobody can audit — and the citation is checked against the flows the node was *given*, not
  against the run, so a five-tuple from somewhere else is refused. The corpus's own rule is why the
  integration fixture is a client whose SNI name is empty on `8443`: `sni_to_app_type` maps a
  non-empty unknown name to `HTTPS`, so an unknown *name* is not `UNKNOWN`, and only a flow that
  offers a fingerprint and no hostname is genuinely unclassified.

### `T5-06` - The maintenance loop and the Critic

- **Status:** done — **Claimed by:** claude-opus-5[1m] — **Session:** ses_t5_06 — **Blocked by:** —
- **Files:** `src/dpi_engine/maintenance.py` (new: `Contradiction`, `Critique`, `Verdict`,
  `critique`, `install`); `src/dpi_agents/maintenance.py` (new: `Proposal`, `review`);
  `src/dpi_agents/nodes/critic.py` (new); `src/dpi_agents/schemas.py` (`parse_critic_verdict`);
  `tests/test_maintenance.py` (new).
- **Test added:** **72** tests in `tests/test_maintenance.py`, across the task's four layers:
  the deterministic falsifier (contradiction rules, canonical order, the five refusals), the
  verdict's invariants, `install`'s two directions, the proposal and the run it names, the parser
  against the computed critique, the node's no-tool surface and prompt, and the loop over a **real
  MCP server** and the corpus's own capture. Every new path is at 100% statement and branch
  coverage.
- **How it was tackled:** **Falsification is a value, not an error path.** `critique` takes the
  proposed signature, every flow under review and the citations the proposal claims, and returns
  the flows the signature matches whose label is a *different known* app. `UNKNOWN` never
  contradicts -- it is the absence of a label -- and a flow already labelled with the proposed app
  is support; the contradictions are sorted by five-tuple so two runs agree. A proposal that cannot
  be cited is refused rather than reviewed: no citation, a duplicate citation, the same flow twice,
  a citation outside the review, or a cited flow that does not hold the signature. `install` is the
  only door and applies the deterministic floor before the model's opinion -- a contradicted
  proposal is refused even with an approving verdict, and a clean one is refused when the Critic
  vetoes. The table's own invariants (unique id, unique conjunction) do the duplicate checks, so
  that rule exists once. On the agent side, `Proposal` binds a candidate to the run that can check
  it, and `review` resolves the citations against that run and hands the whole run to the engine.
  The Critic has **no tools**: its evidence is `Critique.to_json()` passed as the first user
  message, and `parse_critic_verdict` refuses an approval of a contradicted proposal ("rubber
  stamp"), a counterexample that is not one of the contradictions, and a falsification that says
  nothing -- while still allowing the model to veto a clean proposal. Mutation testing then found
  two shape checks in the parser that duplicated `Verdict`'s own invariants with identical
  observable behaviour; they were removed rather than documented as equivalences.
- **Deviations:** two, both in §7: the design's Critic also has `simulate_ruleset`, which this build
  does not have (there is no ruleset store yet), so the node has no tools at all; and the design's
  verdict is `Critique{objections, required_changes, approve}`, while this implementation is
  `Verdict{outcome, reason, objections, counterexamples}`.
- **Verification:** `ruff check .` clean · `mypy --strict` clean on **58** source files ·

  the new schema paths at **100%** statement and branch coverage on every run · mutation harness

### `T5-07` - The evaluation and the phase's exit criteria

- **Status:** done — **Claimed by:** big-pickle — **Session:** ses_t5_07 — **Blocked by:** —
- **Files:** `tests/test_classifier_accuracy.py` (new, 15 tests); **no production code changed**.
  The measurement it installs runs the real pipeline through `tests/golden/scoring.py` and
  `tests/golden/corpus.py`, over `src/dpi_engine/{flow_features,signatures,proposals,maintenance,pipeline,run_store}.py`
  and `src/dpi_agents/{maintenance,schemas,runtime}.py` plus `src/dpi_agents/nodes/critic.py`.
- **Test added:** `tests/test_classifier_accuracy.py` — 15 tests in six groups: the corpus
  coverage, the measured classifier, the forced failures, the fingerprint table as a second
  classifier, the checkability of every accepted proposal, and the phase's exit criterion.
  `TestTheMeasurementCanFail` (4 tests) forces the failure paths by hand, because the healthy
  corpus never reaches them.
- **How it was tackled:** **Measured, not asserted.** `score_corpus` runs the real pipeline over all
  33 captures, and the expectation is **derived** from the labels rather than copied: every label is
  either matched or one of the six named `KNOWN_DIVERGENCES`, so precision is
  `(59 - 6) / 59 = 53/59 = 0.8983`. Measured: recall **1.0**, **29/33** perfect cases, **0**
  unaccepted mismatches, and the six false positives are exactly the allowlist — an accepted
  divergence is still counted as a false positive for the app it *actually* classified as, so the
  figure does not improve by accepting it. The fingerprint table is the **second classifier**: over
  the same 59 labelled flows it must **abstain or agree**, never contradict; measured, it abstains
  on all 59, because its only two published positives (Chrome's JA4, Sliver's JA4S) are not corpus
  traffic.
  **Checkability.** `_assert_checkable` resolves every citation through the run store, recomputes
  the shared facts with `common_predicates`, and adds checks that do not use that function: a
  proposed value must be observed (`None` and `""` refuse), the signature id must be the proposal's,
  its `all_of` must equal the proposed conjunction, and it must match each of its own citations.
  A fabricated five-tuple is refused by `review` outright rather than scored. A corpus-wide sweep
  builds every proposal the corpus can produce (the 33 cases plus the two synthetic gap captures),
  asserts at least one acceptance and at least one contradiction, and re-checks every acceptance.
  **The exit criterion, executed.** gap → `common_predicates` → `Proposal` → `review`
  approves → a scripted Critic approves → `install` returns a new table whose `identify`
  answers `GAP-CLIENT`, while `DEFAULT_TABLE` still abstains (install returns, never mutates). The
  contradicted case is a JA4-only proposal on the mixed capture: the gap client and the
  `netflix.com` flow share the generator's single JA4, so `review` names the Netflix contradiction,
  the Critic falsifies it with that counterexample, and `install` refuses it **even when handed an
  approving verdict**.
  **Test-first.** The file did not exist, so the first run failed with `ERROR: file or directory
  not found`; the first 11 tests then passed. Mutation killed 21 of 25 and two were real gaps: the
  healthy corpus never reaches the unlisted-mismatch path, and nothing forced the allowlist's
  precision. `TestTheMeasurementCanFail` now builds each failure by hand with `score_case` — the
  same scorer, no parallel path — for an unlisted mismatch, an invented detection, an accepted
  divergence that must still count against the actual app, and an allowlist entry that matches a
  case name but not a different hostname. After that: **25/25 killed, 0 survivors, 0 stale**.
- **Deviations:** one, in §7: the corpus has **no unknown case** (recall is total), so the gap the
  agent closes is built from the corpus's own generator with the configuration `T5-05`/`T5-06`
  recorded — an empty SNI on port **8443**. The bytes, the fingerprint, the pipeline and the
  maintenance loop are real; only the label is the absence of one.
- **Verification:** `ruff check .` clean · `mypy --strict` clean on **58** source files ·
  `pytest -q` **1841 passed, 25 skipped** (the 24 differential skips are the missing C++ toolchain)
  · total coverage **93.78%-93.83%** across two runs of this identical tree — the spread is
  the pre-existing multiprocess-sensitive `pipeline.py`/`quic.py`, and this task adds **no source
  lines** (5929 statements / 1806 branches, unchanged) · mutation harness
  `C:\Users\atuls\AppData\Local\Temp\opencode\mutate_t507.py`: **25/25 killed, 0 survivors,
  0 stale**, all eight targets restored byte-for-byte, with four equivalences documented (the
  scoring `accounted` guard is unreachable for this data model; proposals' union and intersection
  coincide on identical flows; the same-app contradiction exclusion is unreachable here;
  `identify`'s conflict rule is `T5-04`'s).
- **Closed findings:** none.
- **Follow-ups:** **Phase 6's `RuleStore`** is where an installed table is written, versioned and
  given provenance. The Critic is exercised against a **scripted** model here, so a real model's
  falsification quality remains `T4-07`'s unset-key path. The fingerprint table's second-classifier
  measurement is a **negative** one because the corpus has a single synthetic TLS client; a corpus
  with more than one TLS stack would make it a real positive measurement.
- **Notes:** The headline is **not 100%**, and that is the point: **53/59** is the honest number,
  because the six substring false positives (`D-27`, accepted as `DEC-07`) are counted rather than
  hidden by the allowlist. The second classifier is measured too — over the corpus it abstains,
  and the rule pinned is *agree or abstain, never contradict*, which is what must survive the first
  real signature. The exit criterion is not a description: the same run that proposes the signature
  installs it and then identifies the flow with it.

### `T6-01` - The `RuleStore`: versioned rulesets, atomic publish, history and restore

- **Status:** pending — **Claimed by:** — **Session:** — **Blocked by:** —
- **Files:** `src/dpi_engine/rule_store.py` (new), `tests/test_rule_store.py` (new); it reuses
  `rule_manager.py`'s loader and `signatures.py`'s table rather than adding a second validator.
- **Test added:** —
- **How it was tackled:** —
- **Deviations:** —
- **Verification:** —
- **Closed findings:** —
- **Follow-ups:** —
- **Notes:** The store is the phase's foundation, so its invariants come first. A version is a
  **policy document** — the blocking ruleset (`RuleManager.to_dict`) or the signature table
  (`SignatureTable.to_json`) — canonicalised and addressed by its own digest, written once under
  `os.replace`, and never overwritten. `restore` is not a rollback of the directory: it commits the
  old content as a *new* version whose parent is the version restored, so a revert is as audible as
  the change it undoes. Validation is delegated to the loader each document already owns; a second
  validator would drift from the first. The active version is a pointer, and only `apply` moves it.

### `T6-02` - The signed proposal: validation, provenance and the digest that binds review to apply

- **Status:** pending — **Claimed by:** — **Session:** — **Blocked by:** —
- **Files:** `src/dpi_engine/proposal.py` (new), `tests/test_proposal.py` (new); it reuses
  `Provenance`, `RuleManager.from_dict`, `SignatureTable.from_json` and `ruleset_fingerprint`.
- **Test added:** —
- **How it was tackled:** —
- **Deviations:** —
- **Verification:** —
- **Closed findings:** —
- **Follow-ups:** —
- **Notes:** `signed` here means **content-bound**, not cryptographically keyed: the proposal
  carries a digest over the canonical document plus its provenance, citations and the
  agent/model/prompt versions, and `apply` recomputes it. That is the property the human gate
  needs — the bytes approved are the bytes applied — and it is testable without key
  management. A keyed signature would add forgery resistance against someone who can already write
  the store, which is a different threat; do not claim it. Validation runs the document's own
  loader **before** the queue, so a malformed proposal is a refused proposal and never a human's
  problem.

  returns is data. The model chooses what to propose and why; the engine's validator and the
  breaker decide whether it is admissible. The prompt says the simulation is a counterfactual and
  not an apply, and that a proposal is reviewed by a human. `simulate_ruleset` is read-only by
  construction (`T1-06`), so granting it does not grant a way to change live rules.

### `T6-04` - The blast-radius circuit breaker: a deterministic tier from a real counterfactual

- **Status:** pending — **Claimed by:** — **Session:** — **Blocked by:** —
- **Files:** `src/dpi_engine/blast_radius.py` (new), `tests/test_blast_radius.py` (new); it reads
  `SimulationReport` from `rules/simulator.py` and does not re-derive any decision.
- **Test added:** —
- **How it was tackled:** —
- **Deviations:** —
- **Verification:** —
- **Closed findings:** —
- **Follow-ups:** —
- **Notes:** A number on a diff is not a decision. The breaker turns `SimulationReport` into a
  tier: **auto-apply** only for signature-table entries, which do not drop traffic and are
  reversible; **one-click** for blocking rules at or below both the flow cap and the percentage
  cap; **explicit confirm** above either, when a configured protected app is touched, or when the
  Critic objected; **refused** when the candidate would forward nothing at all. The defaults come
  from Layer 8's table (0.1% for one-click, 5% as the critical line) and are configuration, not
  constants: a threshold is a policy, and a policy belongs in data. A `total_loss` candidate is
  refused rather than queued with a warning, because there is no blast radius at which "block
  everything" becomes a good idea.

### `T6-05` - The durable approval queue: pause, persist, approve or reject

- **Status:** pending — **Claimed by:** — **Session:** — **Blocked by:** —
- **Files:** `src/dpi_engine/approval_queue.py` (new), `tests/test_approval_queue.py` (new).
- **Test added:** —
- **How it was tackled:** —
- **Deviations:** —
- **Verification:** —
- **Closed findings:** —
- **Follow-ups:** —
- **Notes:** The design's durable checkpointer becomes a **durable request**. The agent run is not
  suspended in memory across a human's lunch break: the proposal is written to the queue and the
  process ends; a later `approve` loads it and the apply path runs. That is the property the
  design wants — Monday's decision does not need Monday's process — obtained without a live
  scheduler. A decision is a state transition on a file (`pending` to `approved` or `rejected`),
  written atomically, idempotent by request id, and carrying the reviewer and the timestamp.
  Approval names the digest it reviewed; the apply path re-derives it, so approving one proposal
  cannot apply another. A rejected request is terminal and cannot be revived by a later approve.

### `T6-06` - Tiered auto-apply and the immutable audit log

- **Status:** pending — **Claimed by:** — **Session:** — **Blocked by:** —
- **Files:** `src/dpi_engine/write_path.py` and `src/dpi_engine/audit_log.py` (new),
  `tests/test_write_path.py` and `tests/test_audit_log.py` (new).
- **Test added:** —
- **How it was tackled:** —
- **Deviations:** —
- **Verification:** —
- **Closed findings:** —
- **Follow-ups:** —
- **Notes:** Two things become one door because they must not be able to disagree. `apply` is the
  only function that commits and the only function that writes an audit entry; a caller cannot
  commit through the store and skip the log. The log is **append-only with a hash chain**: each
  entry names the previous entry's hash, so deleting or editing one breaks `verify_chain`, which
  is the honest meaning of immutable for a file. The auto-apply tier is the one path that does not
  wait for a human, so it is the one that needs the strictest eligibility check: signature-table
  entries only, cited evidence, and a logged entry. Alerting is a reader of this log and belongs
  to the product surface; this task makes the log complete, not the alert. Applying changes the
  store's active version, not a running process; hot-swapping live workers is out of scope.

### `T6-07` - The write path end to end: propose, approve, apply, revert — and the refusals

- **Status:** pending — **Claimed by:** — **Session:** — **Blocked by:** —
- **Files:** `tests/test_write_path.py` (new); no production code expected, and any it needs is a
  hole this task found rather than a feature it planned.
- **Test added:** —
- **How it was tackled:** —
- **Deviations:** —
- **Verification:** —
- **Closed findings:** —
- **Follow-ups:** —
- **Notes:** The phase's exit criterion, executed. A real capture goes through the agent (a
  scripted model, as `T5-07` did), the breaker classifies the proposal from a real counterfactual,
  a scripted reviewer approves it, `apply` commits it, and a run that loads the active version
  blocks what the proposal said it would — the change is visible in the pipeline's own
  decisions, not in a field the test wrote. Then revert: the prior version becomes active *as a
  new version*, the capture is unchanged again, and the audit chain still verifies. The negative
  half is the point of a safety feature: the agent has no write tool, an unapproved apply is
  refused, a substituted digest is refused, a total-loss candidate is refused, and an edited audit
  entry is detected. Nothing here hot-swaps a live process; the phase's apply is to the store's
  active version, and the run that reads it is the proof.

- A finding is `closed` only when its owning task is `done` **and** a regression test exists that fails without the fix.

**Rule:** if a deviation changes a task's scope, estimate, or gate, also update
`IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` and note it here with `Plan updated? = yes`. Do not let the
two documents diverge silently.

## 8. Session log
**Append-only.** One row per working session. This is how continuity works across
sessions and agents — a new session reads this before planning anything.

| Date | Session | Agent | Tasks touched | Outcome | Next action |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2026-10-26 | `ses_t5_07` | big-pickle | Phase 6's decomposition (`T6-01`..`T6-07`) | **Phase 6 is decomposed and ready (0/7)** — seven tasks, in the plan and on the board. The creative decision is the store's shape: a version is a canonical policy document addressed by its own digest, a revert commits the old content as a *new* version, and `apply` is the only door that moves the active pointer and writes the audit entry. The agent gets `query_flows` and `simulate_ruleset` and nothing that writes; the breaker classifies a real counterfactual, and a total-loss candidate is refused rather than made approvable. | `T6-01`: the `RuleStore` — versioned policy documents with provenance, atomic publish, history and restore, and the active version as the only thing `apply` moves. |
| 2026-10-26 | `ses_t5_07` | big-pickle | `T5-07` | **`T5-07` done — Phase 5 complete (7/7)** — the evaluation, and the phase's exit criterion executed rather than described. **The classifier's accuracy is measured, and it is not 100%:** `score_corpus` runs the real pipeline over all 33 captures and all 59 labels at **1.0 recall**, **53/59 = 0.8983 precision**, **29/33 perfect cases** and **zero unaccepted mismatches** — the six false positives are exactly the six `KNOWN_DIVERGENCES`, counted against the app they actually classified as rather than excused by the allowlist, so the figure cannot be improved by accepting a bug. **The fingerprint table is measured as a second classifier:** over the same 59 labelled flows it must abstain or agree, never contradict; it abstains on all 59 because its two published positives (Chrome's JA4, Sliver's JA4S) are not corpus traffic. **Every accepted proposal is checkable:** citations are resolved through the run store, the shared facts are recomputed, an unobserved value and a wrong signature id or conjunction are refused, a fabricated five-tuple is refused by `review` outright, and a corpus-wide sweep asserts at least one acceptance and at least one contradiction. **The exit criterion runs end to end:** gap -> proposal -> review -> Critic approves -> `install` -> `identify` answers `GAP-CLIENT` while `DEFAULT_TABLE` still abstains; the JA4-only proposal on the mixed capture is contradicted by the `netflix.com` flow, the Critic falsifies it with that counterexample, and `install` refuses it **even with an approving verdict**. Test-first (`ERROR: file or directory not found`) -> 11 tests -> mutation killed 21/25 and exposed two tests that could not fail, so `TestTheMeasurementCanFail` now forces an unlisted mismatch, an invented detection, an allowlisted divergence's false positive and a case-name-only allowlist match; **25/25 killed, 0 survivors, 0 stale**, four equivalences documented. 15 new tests, no source lines added (5929/1806 unchanged), 1841 passed / 25 skipped, coverage **93.78%-93.83%** on two runs of the same tree. ## 9. Status vocabulary

Closed set. Only these six values are valid in any `Status` field.

| Status | Meaning | Requires |
|---|---|---|
| `pending` | Not started | nothing |
| `blocked` | Cannot start | `Blocked by:` naming a `DEC-##` |
| `in_progress` | Claimed, work begun | `Claimed by:` and `Session:` |
| `done` | Complete | `Test added`, `Verification` with passing output, `Closed findings` |
| `deferred` | Consciously moved out of scope | `Follow-ups:` saying when it will be revisited |
| `partial` | Some scope done, the remainder blocked or not started. Only valid alongside a `Follow-ups:` naming what remains | `Files:` must distinguish what is done from what is not |

**Transitions that are forbidden:**
- `done` → anything else. If a fix is reverted, create a new task; do not reopen.
- `blocked` → `pending` without recording a decision in §2.
- `dropped` → `pending` without a new deviation-log entry explaining the reversal.

---

## 10. Definition of done

A task is `done` when **all** of these hold:

- [ ] The specified change is implemented
- [ ] A test exists that **fails without the fix and passes with it**
- [ ] `uv run ruff check .` clean
- [ ] `uv run mypy` clean
- [ ] `uv run pytest -q` green, with **no reduction** in the passing count
- [ ] The task's `Traces` findings are marked closed in §6
- [ ] This record's `How it was tackled` and `Deviations` are filled in — **not left blank**
- [ ] §1 Current state, §4 Task board, §6 Findings status and §8 Session log are all updated
- [ ] If a deviation changed scope, `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` is updated and §7 records it

**A defect closed by a code change with no test is not closed.**
`tests/test_pcap_io.py:41`, which asserts the `D-06` bug, is the standing example of
what failure looks like.

---

## Appendix A — Task record template

Copy into §5 when adding a task. Keep field order.

```markdown

### `T<n>-<nn>` — <title>

- **Status:** pending — **Claimed by:** <who> — **Session:** <session id> — **Blocked by:** <DEC-## or ->
- **Files:** <paths>
- **Test added:** <the specific test, or "none needed">
- **How it was tackled:** <what actually happened>
- **Deviations:** <what differed from the plan, and why>
- **Verification:** <commands run + result>
- **Closed findings:** <IDs>
- **Follow-ups:** <anything spawned>
- **Notes:** <non-obvious context>
```

## Appendix B — Phase exit criteria

**Phase 0** — no unbounded allocation reachable from any documented flag · no hang
path without a diagnosable error · green CI on both jobs · coverage ≥ 85 · all 404
tests pass plus new regression tests · `mypy --strict` clean · `ruff format --check`
clean · all 30 tracker task records filled in.

**Phase 0.5** — a VLAN-tagged enterprise capture classifies identically to its
untagged equivalent · pcapng input produces a precise, actionable diagnostic.

**Phase 1** - all 404 existing tests pass unchanged · streaming output deterministic
under the chosen `DEC-01` option · differential parity with the C++ reference intact ·
`simulate_ruleset` working and exercised by a test · memory bounded under a streaming
run with a directory-watch source.

*Verified 2026-10-15 (`ses_t1_04`, completed `ses_t1_04b`), all nine tasks `done`.*
**All five criteria are now met and tested.** Criterion 3 was verified in a second
pass once Docker was running: the suite is **14 passed / 0 skipped**, and the whole
project is **749 passed with nothing skipped** — the first fully-verified run it has
had. `DEC-05` and `DEC-06` remain deliberate, allowlisted divergences (entries 6
and 8) rather than open risks; the differential suite excludes them so parity holds
for everything else, and `tests/test_parity_breaks.py` verifies the port side of
each claim. Criterion 5 was unmeasured until this session and the measurement found a
real defect (`WatchSource` gave every file a fresh `max_total_packets` budget, so a
308-packet run sailed past a limit of 50); it is now tested and fixed.

**Caveat on the differential suite:** it needs a running Docker daemon. A machine
without one silently reports 12 skips, and a green run there is *not* evidence of
parity — the same trap as `--benchmark-enable`.

**Per-release** — `ruff format --check` clean · coverage ≥ 85 · differential suite
green · golden-corpus thresholds met · safety suite green · `AUDIT.md` updated with
any new IDs.
